Q1. Tens digit \(5\) and digit sum \(14\) are fixed. What is the number?
A. \(59\)
B. \(60\)
C. \(58\)
D. \(61\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
5+u=14\Rightarrow u=9
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 59.

\[
N=10\times 5+9=59
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 59 is 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(59\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=9
\]

\[
N=59
\]

Fast result: \(59\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. The tens place has \(3\), and all digits add to \(9\). Which number is it?

\[
N=10\times 3+6=36
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 36 is 9.

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[
u=6
\]

\[
N=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_missing_digit_multi_rule

Q3. The number \(8x216\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q4. Between \(108\) and \(208\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 108 is 108.

\[
6\times 18=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 204 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 208.

\[
208=6\times 34+4
\]

\[
208-4=204
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{204-108}{6}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{208}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{107}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
34-17=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
---
Q5. Between \(124\) and \(212\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 124 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 210 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 212.

\[
212=15\times 14+2
\]

\[
212-2=210
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{210-135}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{212}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{123}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
14-8=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_range_count

Q6. Between \(205\) and \(331\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 205 is 210.

\[
6\times 35=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 331.

\[
331=6\times 55+1
\]

\[
331-1=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{330-210}{6}+1=20+1=21
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{331}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{204}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
55-34=21
\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q7. For two numbers \(117\) and \(90\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(1170\)
B. \(1287\)
C. \(1404\)
D. \(1053\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
117=3^{2}\times 13
\]

\[
90=2\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(117,90)=1170
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1170.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1170\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(117,90)=\frac{117\times 90}{\operatorname{HCF}(117,90)}=1170
\]

Fast result: \(1170\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge

Q8. For the number \(4320\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(47\)
B. \(50\)
C. \(48\)
D. \(49\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
4320=2^{5}\times 3^{3}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(4320)=(5+1)\times (3+1)\times (1+1)=48
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 48.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(48\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (3+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(48\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_hidden_divisor_deduction
---

Q14. For the number \(480\), find the count of odd divisors?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(1+1)\times (1+1)
\]

\[
O=4
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(1+1)\times (1+1)=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_odd_even_divisor_count
---
Q15. For the number \(1440\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(5897\)
B. \(4423\)
C. \(4914\)
D. \(5405\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(1440)=4914
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 4914.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4914\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(4914\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q16. For the number \(20\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(9600\)
B. \(7200\)
C. \(8000\)
D. \(8800\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
20=2^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(20)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 20 d=20^{d(20)/2}=20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 8000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---
Q17. For two numbers \(78\) and \(60\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(780\)
B. \(819\)
C. \(858\)
D. \(741\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=\frac{78\times 60}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,60)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q25. Power \(13^{35}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(35-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
13^{35}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(35-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{35}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division
---
Q26. Power \(2^{94}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(94-1)\bmod 12+1=10
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
2^{94}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 10th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(94-1)\bmod 12+1=10
\]

\[
2^{94}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Cycle length = 12.

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(33-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
11^{33}\equiv 8\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[
p=(33-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

\[
11^{33}\equiv 8\pmod{13}

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q41. With tens \(7\) and ones \(1\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(54\)
B. \(55\)
C. \(53\)
D. \(56\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+1=71
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+7=17
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-1|=9\times 6=54
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-1|=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q52. Between \(204\) and \(372\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 204 is 210.

\[
15\times 14=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 372.

\[
372=15\times 24+12
\]

\[
372-12=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{360-210}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{372}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{203}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-13=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q53. Two buses leave a stand every \(126\) and \(234\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(1474\)
B. \(1638\)
C. \(1802\)
D. \(1966\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
126=2\times 3^{2}\times 7
\]

\[
234=2\times 3^{2}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=1638
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1638.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1638\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=\frac{126\times 234}{\operatorname{HCF}(126,234)}=1638
\]

Fast result: \(1638\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{4}
\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=2
\]

Q88. The number \(836x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 25; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
25+x=27
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
25+2=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,42)=462
\]

\]

Fast result: \(462\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Answer: D

Explanation:

\[

\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q93. When \(2^{52}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(52-1)\bmod 12+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
2^{52}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(52-1)\bmod 12+1=4
\]

\[
2^{52}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q107. Between \(183\) and \(293\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 183 is 195.

\[
15\times 13=195
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 285 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 293.

\[
293=15\times 19+8
\]

\[
293-8=285
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{285-195}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{293}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{182}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-12=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

N=10\times 7+8=78
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 78 is 15.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(78\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=8
\]

\[
N=78
\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[

\]

Trailing zeroes in 75! = 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\[

\[

\]

\[
N=216
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(216\).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(216\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 221 is 234.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 330.

\[
330=18\times 18+6
\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{324-234}{18}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{330}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{220}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-12=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q119. After prime factorising \(18\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(6415\)
B. \(6998\)
C. \(5249\)
D. \(5832\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
18=2\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(18)=(1+1)\times (2+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 18 d=18^{d(18)/2}=18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 5832.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5832\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Fast result: \(5832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q120. Bells ring at intervals of \(63\) and \(63\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?

D. \(64\)
Answer: C

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(63,63)=63
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 63.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(63\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(63,63)=\frac{63\times 63}{\operatorname{HCF}(63,63)}=63
\]

Fast result: \(63\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q122. When \(13^{61}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(61-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
13^{61}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(61-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
3^{61}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q123. Digits \(4\) and \(6\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(19\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+6=46
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+4=64
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-6|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-6|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q143. The number \(30x83\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-2
\]

\[
-2+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-2+x=0
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-2+2=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q146. When \(7^{26}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(26-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
7^{26}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(26-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{26}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q158. Between \(186\) and \(357\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(21\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 186 is 189.

\[
9\times 21=189
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 351 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 357.

\[
357=9\times 39+6
\]

\[
357-6=351
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{351-189}{9}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{357}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{185}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
39-20=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q168. Between \(185\) and \(333\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(24\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(25\)
D. \(26\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 185 is 186.

\[
6\times 31=186
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 333.

\[
333=6\times 55+3
\]

\[
333-3=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{330-186}{6}+1=24+1=25
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 25.
Hence, there are 25 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(25\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{333}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{184}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
55-30=25
\]

Fast result: \(25\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q169. For exponent \(113\), the powers of \(17\) repeat in remainders modulo \(13\). What remainder appears?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
17\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(113-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
17^{113}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(113-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
4^{113}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q178. For two numbers \(24\) and \(78\), which common multiple is first reached?

D. \(296\)
Answer: A

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(24,78)=312
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 312.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(312\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(24,78)=\frac{24\times 78}{\operatorname{HCF}(24,78)}=312
\]

Fast result: \(312\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q188. The number \(3x874\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=8
\]

\[
8-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
8-x=0
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
8-8=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(72-1)\bmod 8+1=8
\]

\[
2^{72}\equiv 1\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q198. The number \(67x92\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 24; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
24+x=27
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
24+3=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q199. For two numbers \(165\) and \(150\), which common multiple is first reached?

B. \(1815\)
C. \(1980\)
D. \(1485\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[

\]

\[
150=2\times 3\times 5^{2}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,150)=1650
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1650.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1650\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,150)=\frac{165\times 150}{\operatorname{HCF}(165,150)}=1650
\]

Fast result: \(1650\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q200. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(32-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
2^{32}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(32-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[
2^{32}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q214. Between \(140\) and \(313\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 140 is 144.

\[
9\times 16=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 313.

\[
313=9\times 34+7
\]

\[
313-7=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{306-144}{9}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{313}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{139}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
34-15=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

B. \(792\)
C. \(684\)
D. \(720\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

\[
m=72
\]

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
q=\left\lfloor\frac{733}{72}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
N=72\times q=720
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 720.
\[
N=720
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(720\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=720
\]

Fast result: \(720\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q225. Tens digit \(8\), digit sum \(10\): which two-digit number satisfies both facts?
A. \(83\)
B. \(81\)
C. \(84\)
D. \(82\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
8+u=10\Rightarrow u=2
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 82.

\[
N=10\times 8+2=82
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 82 is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(82\).

Shortcut

\[
u=2
\]

\[
N=82
\]

Fast result: \(82\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q226. Between \(178\) and \(323\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 178 is 180.

\[
18\times 10=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 323.

\[
323=18\times 17+17
\]

\[
323-17=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{306-180}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{323}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{177}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-9=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q227. Two cyclic events repeat every \(60\) and \(156\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(819\)
B. \(858\)
C. \(741\)
D. \(780\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
156=2^{2}\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,156)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,156)=\frac{60\times 156}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,156)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q229. Power \(7^{70}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(70-1)\bmod 12+1=10
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
7^{70}\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 10th term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(70-1)\bmod 12+1=10
\]

\[
7^{70}\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q243. Number \(85\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(29\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(28\)
D. \(26\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+5=85
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 5+8=58
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-5|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-5|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q249. What is the least perfect cube that is divisible by \(192\)?
A. \(1555\)
B. \(1728\)
C. \(1901\)
D. \(2074\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
192=2^{6}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a cube, every exponent must be a multiple of 3.

\[
2^{6}\to 2^{6}
\]

\[
3^{1}\to 3^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
192\times 9=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=1728
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=9
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 328 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 335.

\[
335=8\times 41+7
\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{328-200}{8}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{335}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{195}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
41-24=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q255. When \(11^{29}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(29-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
11^{29}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(29-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
2^{29}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q281. Between \(136\) and \(312\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 136 is 144.

\[
18\times 8=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 312.

\[
312=18\times 17+6
\]

\[
312-6=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{306-144}{18}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{312}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{135}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-7=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,255)=1020
\]

\]

Fast result: \(1020\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q283. For exponent \(90\), what ending digit does \(2^{90}\) have?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(90-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
2^{90}\equiv 4\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(90-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
2^{90}\equiv 4\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
15+7+3+1=26
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 30! is 26.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(26\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(26\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{4}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=15
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=2
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q306. Power \(3^{28}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?

B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
3\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(28-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
3^{28}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(28-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

\[
3^{28}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
20+6+2=28
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 3 dividing 60! is 28.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 169 is 180.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 261.

\[
261=12\times 21+9
\]

\[
261-9=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{252-180}{12}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{261}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{168}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-14=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q344. The number \(167x9\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=11
\]

\[
11-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
11-x=11
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=0\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
11-0=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q354. Between \(229\) and \(344\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 229 is 240.

\[
12\times 20=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 336 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 344.

\[
344=12\times 28+8
\]

\[
344-8=336
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{336-240}{12}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{344}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{228}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-19=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(49-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{49}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(49-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
7^{49}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_multi_cluster_reasoning

Q366. Between \(136\) and \(283\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 136 is 136.

\[
8\times 17=136
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 280 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 283.

\[
283=8\times 35+3
\]

\[
283-3=280
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{280-136}{8}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{283}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{135}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
35-16=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q367. Bells ring at intervals of \(165\) and \(150\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(1980\)
B. \(1485\)
C. \(1650\)
D. \(1815\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
165=3\times 5\times 11
\]

\[
150=2\times 3\times 5^{2}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,150)=1650
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1650.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1650\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,150)=\frac{165\times 150}{\operatorname{HCF}(165,150)}=1650
\]

Fast result: \(1650\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

D. \(891000\)
Answer: C

Explanation:

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q390. The number \(436x8\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=21
\]

\[
S+x=21+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q392. When \(11^{70}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(70-1)\bmod 12+1=10
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
11^{70}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 10th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(70-1)\bmod 12+1=10
\]

\[
11^{70}\equiv 10\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q399. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(11085466\)
B. \(12597120\)
C. \(15116544\)
D. \(10077696\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 36 d=36^{d(36)/2}=36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q411. Between \(154\) and \(273\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 154 is 160.

\[
8\times 20=160
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 272 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 273.

\[
273=8\times 34+1
\]

\[
273-1=272
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{272-160}{8}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{273}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{153}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
34-19=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q423. Between \(239\) and \(388\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 239 is 240.

\[
8\times 30=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 384 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 388.

\[
388=8\times 48+4
\]

\[
388-4=384
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{384-240}{8}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{388}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{238}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
48-29=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q433. The number \(336x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

7^{83}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(83-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{83}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q447. Between \(162\) and \(293\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 162 is 168.

\[
12\times 14=168
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 293.

\[
293=12\times 24+5
\]

\[
293-5=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{288-168}{12}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{293}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{161}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-13=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

2^{27}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 7th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(27-1)\bmod 10+1=7
\]

\[
2^{27}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(18\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+3=53
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 3+5=35
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-3|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-3|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=78
\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(78\).

Shortcut

\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q479. The number \(214x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 13.

\[
13+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

x=6
\]

Final Answer

\[

\]

Q495. Between \(114\) and \(290\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(30\)
B. \(31\)
C. \(29\)
D. \(32\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 114 is 114.

\[
6\times 19=114
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 290.

\[
290=6\times 48+2
\]

\[
290-2=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{288-114}{6}+1=29+1=30
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 30.
Hence, there are 30 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(30\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{290}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{113}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
48-18=30
\]

Fast result: \(30\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q496. A number leaves the same remainder as \(7^{61}\) when divided by \(19\). What is that remainder?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(61-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{61}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(61-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
7^{61}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q499. The number \(32x07\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=8
\]

\[
8+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
8+x=11
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
8+3=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q500. Two cyclic events repeat every \(198\) and \(108\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1307\)
B. \(1426\)
C. \(1069\)
D. \(1188\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
198=2\times 3^{2}\times 11
\]

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,108)=1188
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1188.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1188\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,108)=\frac{198\times 108}{\operatorname{HCF}(198,108)}=1188
\]

Fast result: \(1188\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q502. Tens digit \(7\) and ones digit \(2\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(44\)
B. \(47\)
C. \(45\)
D. \(46\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+2=72
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+7=27
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-2|=9\times 5=45
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 45.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(45\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-2|=45
\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q513. The number \(414x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q523. The number \(9x561\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=9
\]

\[
9-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
9-x=0
\]

\[
x=9
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=9\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
9-9=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q524. Power \(2^{57}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(57-1)\bmod 10+1=7
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
2^{57}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 7th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(57-1)\bmod 10+1=7
\]

\[
2^{57}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q533. The number \(55x32\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 15; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

C. \(342\)
D. \(360\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
72=2^{3}\times 3^{2}
\]

\[
180=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=360
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 360.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(360\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=\frac{72\times 180}{\operatorname{HCF}(72,180)}=360
\]

Fast result: \(360\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q539. Between \(224\) and \(360\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 224 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 360.

\[
360=15\times 24+0
\]

\[
360-0=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{360-225}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{360}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{223}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-14=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q549. Power \(11^{42}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(12\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(42-1)\bmod 12+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 12.

\[
11^{42}\equiv 12\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=6
\]

\[
6-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
6-x=0
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
6-6=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q560. The number \(558x2\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q569. Between \(190\) and \(368\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 190 is 198.

\[
9\times 22=198
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 368.

\[
368=9\times 40+8
\]

\[
368-8=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{360-198}{9}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{368}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{189}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
40-21=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q578. Between \(215\) and \(340\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 215 is 216.

\[
18\times 12=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 340.

\[
340=18\times 18+16
\]

\[
340-16=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{324-216}{18}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{340}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{214}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-11=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q579. Power \(7^{79}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(79-1)\bmod 12+1=7
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
7^{79}\equiv 6\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 7th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(79-1)\bmod 12+1=7
\]

\[
7^{79}\equiv 6\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q597. The number \(758x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 22.

\[
22+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q606. Between \(184\) and \(337\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(22\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(21\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 184 is 184.

\[
8\times 23=184
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 336 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 337.

\[
337=8\times 42+1
\]

\[
337-1=336
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{336-184}{8}+1=19+1=20
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(20\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{337}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{183}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
42-22=20
\]

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q609. When \(7^{44}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(44-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{44}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(44-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

\[
7^{44}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

Q628. The number \(7x658\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 26; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
26+x=27
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
26+1=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(26-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{26}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(26-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

\[
3^{26}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q635. The number \(70x85\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=4
\]

\[
4+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
4+x=11
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
4+7=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(35\)
B. \(38\)
C. \(36\)
D. \(37\)
Answer: C

Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+1=51
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+5=15
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-1|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-1|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q651. Between \(178\) and \(281\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 178 is 180.

\[
18\times 10=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 281.

\[
281=18\times 15+11
\]

\[
281-11=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{270-180}{18}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{281}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{177}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-9=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[

\[

\[

Q665. The number \(554x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).
