Q668. Between \(210\) and \(347\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 210 is 216.

\[
12\times 18=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 336 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 347.

\[
347=12\times 28+11
\]

\[
347-11=336
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{336-216}{12}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{347}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{209}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-17=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q682. The number \(970x4\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=20
\]

\[
S+x=20+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

B. \(1092\)

D. \(1310\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
84=2^{2}\times 3\times 7
\]

\[
156=2^{2}\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(84,156)=1092
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1092.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1092\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(84,156)=\frac{84\times 156}{\operatorname{HCF}(84,156)}=1092
\]

Fast result: \(1092\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\[

Q688. Between \(161\) and \(285\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(20\)
B. \(23\)
C. \(21\)
D. \(22\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 161 is 162.

\[
6\times 27=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 282 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 285.

\[
285=6\times 47+3
\]

\[
285-3=282
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{282-162}{6}+1=20+1=21
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{285}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{160}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
47-26=21
\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q692. The number \(332x4\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 12; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
12+x=18
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
12+6=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[

\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]

Final Answer

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q711. Between \(135\) and \(231\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(14\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 135 is 136.

\[
8\times 17=136
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 224 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 231.

\[
231=8\times 28+7
\]

\[
231-7=224
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{224-136}{8}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{231}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{134}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-16=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(25-1)\bmod 10+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
2^{25}\equiv 10\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(25-1)\bmod 10+1=5
\]

\[
2^{25}\equiv 10\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q719. The number \(114x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 14; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q729. The number \(836x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[

A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(49-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
2^{49}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(49-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
2^{49}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q753. The number \(8x907\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=24
\]

\[
24-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 22.

\[
24-x=22
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
24-2=22
\]
\[
22\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q760. The number \(53x58\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 21; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[

\]

Final Answer

Q772. Power \(7^{81}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(81-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{81}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(81-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
7^{81}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q780. The number \(592x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 22.

\[
22+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification

\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

(87-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
7^{87}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(87-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{87}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q794. The number \(4x905\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=18
\]

\[
18-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
18-x=11
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
18-7=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Q797. Between \(191\) and \(357\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(28\)
B. \(29\)
C. \(27\)
D. \(30\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 191 is 192.

\[
6\times 32=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 354 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 357.

\[
357=6\times 59+3
\]

\[
357-3=354
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{354-192}{6}+1=27+1=28
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 28.
Hence, there are 28 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{357}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{190}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
59-31=28
\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q808. The number \(170x2\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=10
\]

\[
S+x=10+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
10+x=18
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
10+8=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 207 is 216.

\[
12\times 18=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 290.

\[
290=12\times 24+2
\]

\[
290-2=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{288-216}{12}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{290}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{206}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-17=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q825. The number \(114x8\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=14
\]

\[
S+x=14+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,90)=450
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 450.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(450\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,90)=\frac{75\times 90}{\operatorname{HCF}(75,90)}=450
\]

Fast result: \(450\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q832. The number \(610x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 13.

\[
13+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[

\[

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=4
\]

\[
4+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
4+x=11
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
4+7=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

Q869. The tens place has \(7\), and all digits add to \(14\). Which number is it?
A. \(77\)
B. \(78\)
C. \(76\)
D. \(79\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
7+u=14\Rightarrow u=7
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 77.

\[
N=10\times 7+7=77
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 77 is 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(77\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=77
\]

Fast result: \(77\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q870. A large power \(11^{110}\) is divided by \(9\). What remainder should be written?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(110-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{110}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(110-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[
2^{110}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q873. The number \(95x72\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=23
\]

\[
S+x=23+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
12+2=14
\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q880. Between \(123\) and \(256\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 123 is 132.

\[
12\times 11=132
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 256.

\[
256=12\times 21+4
\]

\[
256-4=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{252-132}{12}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{256}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{122}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-10=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q887. The number \(71x38\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
23\equiv 23\pmod{100}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 20.

\[
23\rightarrow 29\rightarrow 67\rightarrow 41\rightarrow 43\rightarrow 89\rightarrow 47\rightarrow 81\rightarrow 63\rightarrow 49\rightarrow 27\rightarrow 21\rightarrow 83\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 61\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 69\rightarrow 87\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(108-1)\bmod 20+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 81.

\[
23^{108}\equiv 81\pmod{100}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 81.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(81\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(108-1)\bmod 20+1=8
\]

\[
23^{108}\equiv 81\pmod{100}
\]

Fast result: \(81\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q904. The number \(25x72\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
13+4+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 3 dividing 40! is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{3}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{9}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q914. Between \(145\) and \(312\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(22\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(23\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 145 is 152.

\[
8\times 19=152
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 312.

\[
312=8\times 39+0
\]

\[
312-0=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{312-152}{8}+1=20+1=21
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{312}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{144}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
39-18=21
\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q924. The number \(6x650\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q925. When \(11^{29}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(29-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
11^{29}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(29-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
4^{29}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{8}\right\rfloor

\]

\[

\]

Highest power of 2 dividing 50! is 47.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(47\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(47\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q930. Between \(210\) and \(324\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 210 is 216.

\[
9\times 24=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 324.

\[
324=9\times 36+0
\]

\[
324-0=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{324-216}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{324}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{209}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-23=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q934. The number \(3x561\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=3
\]

\[
3-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
3-x=0
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
3-3=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q951. The number \(97x94\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 29; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
29+x=36
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
29+7=36
\]
\[
36\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q952. When \(2^{24}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(24-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
2^{24}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(24-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

\[
2^{24}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q954. Between \(114\) and \(273\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 114 is 120.

\[
15\times 8=120
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 273.

\[
273=15\times 18+3
\]

\[
273-3=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-120}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{273}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{113}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-7=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q958. The number \(970x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q975. The number \(31x36\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 13; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q976. When \(7^{46}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(46-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
7^{46}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(46-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
2^{46}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q982. The number \(236x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 11.

\[
11+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
11+x=15
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
11+4=15
\]
\[
15\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q990. Between \(177\) and \(316\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 177 is 184.

\[
8\times 23=184
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 316.

\[
316=8\times 39+4
\]

\[
316-4=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{312-184}{8}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{316}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{176}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
39-22=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 25.

\[
25+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
25+x=30
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
25+5=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q1000. Tens digit \(8\) and digit sum \(15\) are fixed. What is the number?
A. \(88\)
B. \(86\)
C. \(89\)
D. \(87\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
8+u=15\Rightarrow u=7
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 87.

\[
N=10\times 8+7=87
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 87 is 15.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(87\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=87
\]

Fast result: \(87\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_sum_of_digits
